Hi. Um, well, I came in because... lately, once I start eating, it feels like I just disappear. Like something else entirely takes over. It's kind of like autopilot, you know? Control? No. Zero. None at all. It's honestly like... you know when your controller disconnects right in the middle of a match? You're just mashing the buttons in your head and nothing is happening. 

Which is the absolute worst, by the way. My roommate last semester, he'd always leave the PS5 controllers off the charging dock. Like, how hard is it to just click it into place when you're done? But no, he'd leave them on the couch, usually under a pillow or a hoodie or something, and then I'd log on to play with my friends from back home, and right as the game starts, boom, battery dead. But um, what were we talking about? Oh, right. The food. Yeah, I don't really have any say in what I'm eating or how much once that autopilot kicks in. I'm just kind of watching it happen.

Um, no? I mean, I don't think so. Like I said, I haven't really been doing anything where that would even come up. It's just not on my radar right now.

Um, I don't know? I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess I just figure I'm too stressed out with everything else to care about dating or hooking up or whatever. 

Like, my eating habits... I don't know, it's just food, you know? It's not like food is what's keeping me from swiping on Tinder. So yeah, between the meal plan app being garbage and my phone screen being completely destroyed, I'm just... not really focused on anything else. I don't think food has anything to do with it, honestly. I'm just busy.

I mean, I really don't think so. Like I said, it's just... I'm just not thinking about it. My eating is just my eating, you know? It's just something I have to deal with, like everything else.

Um, yeah, I guess I do. Like, especially when I'm super stressed about classes. There have been nights after studying where I just... I don't know, I go from the dining hall back to my room and just eat. Like, burgers, bowls of cereal, peanut butter straight out of the jar, chips, literally whatever I can get my hands on. It's way more than a normal dinner, and I just keep going until the trash can is overflowing and I feel physically sick. 

It's actually kind of a problem because my dorm trash can is one of those tiny plastic ones, you know? The ones that fit under a desk but can barely hold a single pizza box. I don't know why they give us those. And the main dumpster is all the way on the other side of the courtyard, which is just brilliant planning by the university. So when it rains, you have to trudge through this massive mud puddle just to throw away your garbage. 

Last week, I tried to take my trash out, and the bag ripped right at the bottom of the stairs. Just... garbage everywhere. And of course, it was right when the girl from my English seminar was walking by. The one who always wears those vintage band tees and has really strong opinions about Sylvia Plath. I had to awkwardly scoop up all these empty wrappers while she just stared at me. It was humiliating. 

But yeah, I definitely eat a lot more when I'm stressed out. It just kind of happens.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, I don't know if it actually helps. Maybe for like, a second? While I'm doing it, I'm not thinking about all the stuff I have to do, but then right after, I just feel gross and even more stressed out. 

It's like when I tried to fix that stupid phone screen myself. I bought one of those DIY repair kits off Amazon because the guy at the mall kiosk wanted like, two hundred bucks, which is insane. So I get the kit, and it comes with these tiny little screwdrivers that look like they belong in a dollhouse. I spent three hours watching this YouTube tutorial by a guy who kept talking about his cryptocurrency investments while taking apart an iPhone. I finally got the screen off, but then I sneezed and lost one of the microscopic screws in the carpet. I spent another hour crawling around with a flashlight, and I never found it. So now my phone is held together with a piece of clear tape in the top corner, and the front camera is permanently blurry, which makes all my selfies look like they were taken in a steam room. 

So yeah, I guess eating is kind of like that. It seems like a good idea to fix the stress in the moment, but then it just makes everything worse and messier.

I was doing a pure mage build, which is actually really hard early game because your magicka regenerates so slowly and you basically have to run away from everything. I spent like two days trying to troubleshoot it, going through all the load orders and the text files, and nothing worked. I ended up having to wipe the whole drive and reinstall Windows just to be safe because my computer started making this weird clicking noise. \n\nWhich, by the way, I think might be the power supply failing. If I have to buy a new power supply on top of fixing my phone screen, I'm literally going to have to eat ramen for the rest of the semester. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no, no problems with that. Just... haven't been using it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your interest in sex?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess? I mean, like I've been saying, I just haven't been interested in it at all lately. It's just not something I think about. \n\nHonestly, the only thing I've been interested in lately is trying to figure out how to fix my sleep schedule. It's completely messed up. I've been staying up until like 4 AM watching these YouTube video essays about speedrunning. Have you ever watched those? They're weirdly fascinating. There's this one guy who spent like three years trying to shave two seconds off a Super Mario 64 world record, and he literally mapped out the exact pixel coordinates of where Mario's foot needs to land on a specific frame. It's insane. It makes you realize how much free time some people have. \n\nWhich is ironic, because I feel like I have zero free time between classes and studying, even though I'm basically failing my chemistry lab. My lab partner is this guy who never shows up, and when he does, he just copies my data and leaves. I tried complaining to the TA, but she just told me that \"learning to work with difficult people is part of the college experience.\" Like, no, doing all the work for a group project while some guy named Chad gets an easy A is not the college experience I signed up for. \n\nBut yeah, sex just... isn't really on my mind. I don't know, maybe? Like I said, it kind of distracts me for a minute, but I wouldn't say it actually calms me down. It's more just something I do. 

Kind of like how my mom always starts aggressively organizing the pantry whenever she's mad at my dad. She doesn't even say anything, she just starts taking out all the canned goods and lining them up by expiration date. Last Thanksgiving, they got into an argument about whether to brine the turkey—my dad is anti-brine, which is a whole other thing—and next thing I know, my mom is in the kitchen with a label maker, reorganizing the spice rack. She made a separate section just for different types of paprika. Who even needs three kinds of paprika? Smoked, sweet, Hungarian... it's just red dust. 

But anyway, she claims it helps her process her feelings, but she usually just ends up yelling at someone for putting the garlic powder next to the onion powder. So I guess people just have weird ways of dealing with stuff. But for me, eating just makes me feel worse afterward.

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean... yeah, probably. Like, I don't really sit down and think, "I am sad, so I will eat," but it's definitely tied to my emotions. It's just a way to cope, I guess? Or not cope, since it doesn't work. 

It reminds me of my roommate, honestly. His coping mechanism for literally anything is playing this one specific video game. It's this farming simulator thing, and whenever he bombs a test or gets in a fight with his girlfriend, he just sits there for hours, planting virtual turnips. He actually missed a midterm last month because his digital cow was giving birth or something. I tried to tell him that maybe he should focus on real life, but he got super defensive and told me I didn't understand the "in-game economy." Which, fine, I don't, but I also don't think turnips are going to pass Chem 101 for him. 

But yeah, I don't know if I'm dealing with my emotions when I eat. I think I'm just trying to run away from them for a little bit, even if it ends up backfiring.

I've been staying up until like 4 AM watching these YouTube video essays about speedrunning. Have you ever watched those? They're weirdly fascinating. There's this one guy who spent like three years trying to shave two seconds off a Super Mario 64 world record, and he literally mapped out the exact pixel coordinates of where Mario's foot needs to land on a specific frame. My lab partner is this guy who never shows up, and when he does, he just copies my data and leaves. I tried complaining to the TA, but she just told me that \"learning to work with difficult people is part of the college experience.\" Like, no, doing all the work for a group project while some guy named Chad gets an easy A is not the college experience I signed up for. \n\nBut yeah, sex just... isn't really on my mind. At all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits have anything to do with your lack of interest in sex?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know? I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess I just figure I'm too stressed out with everything else to care about dating or hooking up or whatever. \n\nLike, my eating habits... I don't know, it's just food, you know? It's not like food is what's keeping me from swiping on Tinder. It's more like... the whole dining hall situation is just so stressful anyway. They changed the meal plan system this semester, and now everything is on this weird point system instead of just swipes, and it makes zero sense. You get like, 500 points a week, but a single slice of pizza is somehow 12 points, and a salad is 18 points. Who prices a salad higher than pizza? It's like they want us to be unhealthy. I'm just busy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in sex might be related to how you've been feeling about your eating?\n\nPatient: I mean, I really don't think so. Like I said, it's just... I'm just not thinking about it. My eating is just my eating, you know? It's just something I have to deal with, like everything else. \n\nSpeaking of dealing with things, I actually have to figure out how I'm going to get my stuff out of my dorm at the end of the semester. Have you ever tried to pack up a whole dorm room into a two-door Honda Civic? Because that's what I'm looking at right now. My roommate, the one who leaves his wet towels on my desk chair—which is still happening, by the way, I literally sat on a damp towel yesterday while trying to write a paper on the French Revolution—anyway, he's getting his parents to bring an entire U-Haul just for his stuff. A U-Haul! For one half of a tiny room! He has this massive mini-fridge that he never even cleans out. I swear there's a yogurt in there from September that's probably achieved sentience by now. \n\nAnd my parents aren't coming to help me move out because my dad is weirdly obsessed with this new lawn care routine. He bought this aerator machine and he's been spending every weekend just punching holes in the grass. He sends me pictures of the lawn every Sunday morning. Like, \"Look at the soil drainage, son!\" I don't know what I'm supposed to say to that. \n\nSo yeah, I'm just going to have to cram everything into my car and hope I don't get pulled over because I won't be able to see out the back window. But no, I don't think my eating has anything to do with not wanting to hook up with anyone. I just have way too much other stuff going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat more when you're feeling stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess I do. Like, especially when I'm super stressed about classes. There have been nights after studying where I just... I don't know, I go from the dining hall back to my room and just eat. Like, burgers, bowls of cereal, peanut butter straight out of the jar, chips, literally whatever I can get my hands on. It's way more than a normal dinner, and I just keep going until the trash can is overflowing and I feel physically sick. \n\nIt's actually kind of a problem because my dorm trash can is one of those tiny plastic ones, you know? The ones that fit under a desk but can barely hold a single pizza box. I don't know why they give us those. And the main dumpster is all the way on the other side of the courtyard, which is just brilliant planning by the university. So when it rains, you have to trudge through this massive mud puddle just to throw away your garbage. \n\nLast week, I tried to take my trash out, and the bag ripped right at the bottom of the stairs. Just... garbage everywhere. And of course, it was right when the girl from my English seminar was walking by. The one who always wears those vintage band tees and has really strong opinions about Sylvia Plath. I had to awkwardly scoop up all these empty wrappers while she just stared at me. It was humiliating. \n\nBut yeah, I definitely eat a lot more when I'm stressed out. It just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating helps you feel better when you're stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't know if it actually helps. Maybe for like, a second? While I'm doing it, I'm not thinking about all the stuff I have to do, but then right after, I just feel gross and even more stressed out. \n\nIt's like when I tried to fix that stupid phone screen myself. I bought one of those DIY repair kits off Amazon because the guy at the mall kiosk wanted like, two hundred bucks, which is insane. So I get the kit, and it comes with these tiny little screwdrivers that look like they belong in a dollhouse. I spent three hours watching this YouTube tutorial by a guy who kept talking about his cryptocurrency investments while taking apart an iPhone. I finally got the screen off, but then I sneezed and lost one of the microscopic screws in the carpet. I spent another hour crawling around with a flashlight, and I never found it. So now my phone is held together with a piece of clear tape in the top corner, and the front camera is permanently blurry, which makes all my selfies look like they were taken in a steam room. \n\nSo yeah, I guess eating is kind of like that. It seems like a good idea to fix the stress in the moment, but then it just makes everything worse and messier.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat when you're stressed as a way to calm down or feel better?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? Like I said, it kind of distracts me for a minute, but I wouldn't say it actually calms me down. It's more just something I do. \n\nKind of like how my mom always starts aggressively organizing the pantry whenever she's mad at my dad. She doesn't even say anything, she just starts taking out all the canned goods and lining them up by expiration date. Last Thanksgiving, they got into an argument about whether to brine the turkey—my dad is anti-brine, which is a whole other thing—and next thing I know, my mom is in the kitchen with a label maker, reorganizing the spice rack. She made a separate section just for different types of paprika. Who even needs three kinds of paprika? Smoked, sweet, Hungarian... it's just red dust. \n\nBut anyway, she claims it helps her process her feelings, but she usually just ends up yelling at someone for putting the garlic powder next to the onion powder. So I guess people just have weird ways of dealing with stuff. It was humiliating. \n\nBut yeah, I definitely eat a lot more when I'm stressed out. It just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating helps you feel better when you're stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't know if it actually helps. Maybe for like, a second? While I'm doing it, I'm not thinking about all the stuff I have to do, but then right after, I just feel gross and even more stressed out. \n\nIt's like when I tried to fix that stupid phone screen myself. So I get the kit, and it comes with these tiny little screwdrivers that look like they belong in a dollhouse. Intentionally? Uh, I am not really sure if it is a conscious choice, you know? Like I do not sit there and map out a plan to distract myself. It just sort of happens. 

It is kind of like last semester when I had this massive history paper due on the Industrial Revolution. I knew I was super stressed about it, so I decided to take a break to clear my head. I thought I would just watch one episode of a documentary about deep sea squids. But then I went down this massive rabbit hole about giant squids and how they fight sperm whales, and suddenly it is four in the morning, and I am reading conspiracy theories about giant prehistoric sharks still living in the Mariana Trench. I ended up writing three pages of my history paper about maritime myths before I realized what I was doing. My professor actually left a note on my paper asking why I spent a whole paragraph talking about sea monsters in an essay about textile mills. 

So yeah, I really do not know if I am eating on purpose to distract myself. I think my brain just kind of hijacks me when I am stressed and I end up doing things without really meaning to.

It's way more than a normal dinner, and I just keep going until the trash can is overflowing and I feel physically sick. \n\nIt's actually kind of a problem because my dorm trash can is one of those tiny plastic ones, you know? The ones that fit under a desk but can barely hold a single pizza box. I don't know why they give us those. And the main dumpster is all the way on the other side of the courtyard, which is just brilliant planning by the university. So when it rains, you have to trudge through this massive mud puddle just to throw away your garbage. \n\nLast week, I tried to take my trash out, and the bag ripped right at the bottom of the stairs. Just... garbage everywhere. And of course, it was right when the girl from my English seminar was walking by. The one who always wears those vintage band tees and has really strong opinions about Sylvia Plath. I had to awkwardly scoop up all these empty wrappers while she just stared at me. It was humiliating. \n\nBut yeah, I definitely eat a lot more when I'm stressed out. It just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating helps you feel better when you're stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't know if it actually helps. Maybe for like, a second? While I'm doing it, I'm not thinking about all the stuff I have to do, but then right after, I just feel gross and even more stressed out. \n\nIt's like when I tried to fix that stupid phone screen myself. I bought one of those DIY repair kits off Amazon because the guy at the mall kiosk wanted like, two hundred bucks, which is insane. So I get the kit, and it comes with these tiny little screwdrivers that look like they belong in a dollhouse. I spent three hours watching this YouTube tutorial by a guy who kept talking about his cryptocurrency investments while taking apart an iPhone. I finally got the screen off, but then I sneezed and lost one of the microscopic screws in the carpet. I spent another hour crawling around with a flashlight, and I never found it. So now my phone is held together with a piece of clear tape in the top corner, and the front camera is permanently blurry, which makes all my selfies look like they were taken in a steam room. \n\nSo yeah, I guess eating is kind of like that. It seems like a good idea to fix the stress in the moment, but then it just makes everything worse and messier.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat when you're stressed as a way to calm down or feel better?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? Like I said, it kind of distracts me for a minute, but I wouldn't say it actually calms me down. It's more just something I do. \n\nKind of like how my mom always starts aggressively organizing the pantry whenever she's mad at my dad. She doesn't even say anything, she just starts taking out all the canned goods and lining them up by expiration date. Last Thanksgiving, they got into an argument about whether to brine the turkey—my dad is anti-brine, which is a whole other thing—and next thing I know, my mom is in the kitchen with a label maker, reorganizing the spice rack. She made a separate section just for different types of paprika. Who even needs three kinds of paprika? Smoked, sweet, Hungarian... it's just red dust. \n\nBut anyway, she claims it helps her process her feelings, but she usually just ends up yelling at someone for putting the garlic powder next to the onion powder. So I guess people just have weird ways of dealing with stuff. But for me, eating just makes me feel worse afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat as a way to deal with your emotions?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, probably. Like, I don't really sit down and think, \"I am sad, so I will eat,\" but it's definitely tied to my emotions. It's just a way to cope, I guess? Or not cope, since it doesn't work. \n\nIt reminds me of my roommate, honestly. His coping mechanism for literally anything is playing this one specific video game. It's this farming simulator thing, and whenever he bombs a test or gets in a fight with his girlfriend, he just sits there for hours, planting virtual turnips. He actually missed a midterm last month because his digital cow was giving birth or something. I tried to tell him that maybe he should focus on real life, but he got super defensive and told me I didn't understand the \"in-game economy.\" Which, fine, I don't, but I also don't think turnips are going to pass Chem 101 for him. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if I'm dealing with my emotions when I eat. I think I'm just trying to run away from them for a little bit, even if it ends up backfiring.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat as a way to intentionally distract yourself from feeling upset or stressed?\n\nPatient: Intentionally? Uh, I am not really sure if it is a conscious choice, you know? Like I do not sit there and map out a plan to distract myself. It just sort of happens. \n\nIt is kind of like last semester when I had this massive history paper due on the Industrial Revolution. I knew I was super stressed about it, so I decided to take a break to clear my head. I thought I would just watch one episode of a documentary about deep sea squids. But then I went down this massive rabbit hole about giant squids and how they fight sperm whales, and suddenly it is four in the morning, and I am reading conspiracy theories about giant prehistoric sharks still living in the Mariana Trench. I ended up writing three pages of my history paper about maritime myths before I realized what I was doing. My professor actually left a note on my paper asking why I spent a whole paragraph talking about sea monsters in an essay about textile mills. \n\nSo yeah, I really do not know if I am eating on purpose to distract myself. I think my brain just kind of hijacks me when I am stressed and I end up doing things without really meaning to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't know if I'm trying to avoid them, exactly. It's more like... I just don't want to deal with them right then? Like, I'm not trying to run away forever, just hitting pause. 

Kind of like how my grandpa used to "fix" the lawnmower. Every time it broke, he wouldn't actually take it apart to see what was wrong. He'd just hit it with a rubber mallet a few times, spray WD-40 on whatever part looked rusty, and hope for the best. And sometimes it actually worked! But most of the time, it would just start smoking halfway through the yard, and he'd end up leaving it in the middle of the grass for a week. My grandma would get so mad because the neighbors would stare at this half-mowed lawn with a smoking machine just sitting there. He eventually tried to paint it bright red to make it look newer, but he used indoor paint, so the first time it rained, it just peeled off in these weird, sticky strips. 

But yeah, I don't know if I'm avoiding my feelings. I think I just don't really know what to do with them, so I just... do something else instead.

Honestly, I haven't even been trying to meet anyone lately anyway because my phone screen is completely shattered. I dropped it right outside the dining hall on Tuesday. Have you ever tried using a dating app when your screen looks like a literal spiderweb? It's impossible. You try to swipe on someone and end up clicking some weird pop-up ad for a mobile game. \n\nAnd getting it fixed is such a scam. I went to one of those little kiosks at the mall, and the guy wanted two hundred bucks just to replace the glass! Two hundred! I told him I could buy a decent used graphics card for that kind of money. Well, maybe not a top-tier one, the GPU market is still pretty wild right now. My buddy from my comp sci class tried to buy a used 3060 off eBay last month and the seller literally mailed him a brick in a cardboard box. Like, an actual red brick. eBay refunded him, obviously, but still, the hassle of it all is just ridiculous. \n\nBut um, yeah. No issues with my sex life or anything. Just... haven't really been focused on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty getting or maintaining an erection?\n\nPatient: Um, no? I mean, I don't think so. Like I said, I haven't really been doing anything where that would even come up. It's just not on my radar right now. \n\nHonestly, the only thing I'm trying to get up and running right now is my PC. I was trying to install this new mod for Skyrim the other day—have you ever played Skyrim? I know it's super old, but the modding community is still insane. Anyway, I downloaded this massive overhaul mod, like 40 gigabytes, and it completely broke my save file. Just corrupted the whole thing. I had like three hundred hours on that character. I was doing a pure mage build, which is actually really hard early game because your magicka regenerates so slowly and you basically have to run away from everything. I spent like two days trying to troubleshoot it, going through all the load orders and the text files, and nothing worked. I ended up having to wipe the whole drive and reinstall Windows just to be safe because my computer started making this weird clicking noise. \n\nWhich, by the way, I think might be the power supply failing. If I have to buy a new power supply on top of fixing my phone screen, I'm literally going to have to eat ramen for the rest of the semester. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no, no problems with that. Just... haven't been using it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your interest in sex?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess? I mean, like I've been saying, I just haven't been interested in it at all lately. It's just not something I think about. \n\nHonestly, the only thing I've been interested in lately is trying to figure out how to fix my sleep schedule. It's completely messed up. I've been staying up until like 4 AM watching these YouTube video essays about speedrunning. Have you ever watched those? They're weirdly fascinating. There's this one guy who spent like three years trying to shave two seconds off a Super Mario 64 world record, and he literally mapped out the exact pixel coordinates of where Mario's foot needs to land on a specific frame. It's insane. It makes you realize how much free time some people have. \n\nWhich is ironic, because I feel like I have zero free time between classes and studying, even though I'm basically failing my chemistry lab. My lab partner is this guy who never shows up, and when he does, he just copies my data and leaves. I tried complaining to the TA, but she just told me that \"learning to work with difficult people is part of the college experience.\" Like, no, doing all the work for a group project while some guy named Chad gets an easy A is not the college experience I signed up for. \n\nBut yeah, sex just... isn't really on my mind. At all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits have anything to do with your lack of interest in sex?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know? I haven't really thought about it like that. They changed the meal plan system this semester, and now everything is on this weird point system instead of just swipes, and it makes zero sense. You get like, 500 points a week, but a single slice of pizza is somehow 12 points, and a salad is 18 points. Who prices a salad higher than pizza? It's like they want us to be unhealthy. And the app you have to use to check your balance crashes constantly. I tried to buy a coffee yesterday and stood at the register for five minutes while the little loading circle just spun around and around. The barista was looking at me like I was trying to steal a three-dollar iced latte. \n\nSo yeah, between the meal plan app being garbage and my phone screen being completely destroyed, I'm just... not really focused on anything else. I don't think food has anything to do with it, honestly. I'm just busy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in sex might be related to how you've been feeling about your eating?\n\nPatient: I mean, I really don't think so. Like I said, it's just... I'm just not thinking about it. My eating is just my eating, you know? It's just something I have to deal with, like everything else. \n\nSpeaking of dealing with things, I actually have to figure out how I'm going to get my stuff out of my dorm at the end of the semester. Have you ever tried to pack up a whole dorm room into a two-door Honda Civic? Because that's what I'm looking at right now. My roommate, the one who leaves his wet towels on my desk chair—which is still happening, by the way, I literally sat on a damp towel yesterday while trying to write a paper on the French Revolution—anyway, he's getting his parents to bring an entire U-Haul just for his stuff. A U-Haul! For one half of a tiny room! He has this massive mini-fridge that he never even cleans out. I swear there's a yogurt in there from September that's probably achieved sentience by now. \n\nAnd my parents aren't coming to help me move out because my dad is weirdly obsessed with this new lawn care routine. He bought this aerator machine and he's been spending every weekend just punching holes in the grass. He sends me pictures of the lawn every Sunday morning. Like, \"Look at the soil drainage, son!\" I don't know what I'm supposed to say to that. \n\nSo yeah, I'm just going to have to cram everything into my car and hope I don't get pulled over because I won't be able to see out the back window. But no, I don't think my eating has anything to do with not wanting to hook up with anyone. I just have way too much other stuff going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat more when you're feeling stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess I do. Like, especially when I'm super stressed about classes. There have been nights after studying where I just... I don't know, I go from the dining hall back to my room and just eat. Like, burgers, bowls of cereal, peanut butter straight out of the jar, chips, literally whatever I can get my hands on. It's way more than a normal dinner, and I just keep going until the trash can is overflowing and I feel physically sick. \n\nIt's actually kind of a problem because my dorm trash can is one of those tiny plastic ones, you know? The ones that fit under a desk but can barely hold a single pizza box. I don't know why they give us those. And the main dumpster is all the way on the other side of the courtyard, which is just brilliant planning by the university. So when it rains, you have to trudge through this massive mud puddle just to throw away your garbage. \n\nLast week, I tried to take my trash out, and the bag ripped right at the bottom of the stairs. Just... garbage everywhere. And of course, it was right when the girl from my English seminar was walking by. The one who always wears those vintage band tees and has really strong opinions about Sylvia Plath. I had to awkwardly scoop up all these empty wrappers while she just stared at me. It was humiliating. \n\nBut yeah, I definitely eat a lot more when I'm stressed out. It just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating helps you feel better when you're stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't know if it actually helps. Maybe for like, a second? While I'm doing it, I'm not thinking about all the stuff I have to do, but then right after, I just feel gross and even more stressed out. \n\nIt's like when I tried to fix that stupid phone screen myself. I bought one of those DIY repair kits off Amazon because the guy at the mall kiosk wanted like, two hundred bucks, which is insane. So I get the kit, and it comes with these tiny little screwdrivers that look like they belong in a dollhouse. I spent three hours watching this YouTube tutorial by a guy who kept talking about his cryptocurrency investments while taking apart an iPhone. I finally got the screen off, but then I sneezed and lost one of the microscopic screws in the carpet. I spent another hour crawling around with a flashlight, and I never found it. So now my phone is held together with a piece of clear tape in the top corner, and the front camera is permanently blurry, which makes all my selfies look like they were taken in a steam room. \n\nSo yeah, I guess eating is kind of like that. It seems like a good idea to fix the stress in the moment, but then it just makes everything worse and messier.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat when you're stressed as a way to calm down or feel better?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? Like I said, it kind of distracts me for a minute, but I wouldn't say it actually calms me down. It's more just something I do. \n\nKind of like how my mom always starts aggressively organizing the pantry whenever she's mad at my dad. She doesn't even say anything, she just starts taking out all the canned goods and lining them up by expiration date. Last Thanksgiving, they got into an argument about whether to brine the turkey—my dad is anti-brine, which is a whole other thing—and next thing I know, my mom is in the kitchen with a label maker, reorganizing the spice rack. She made a separate section just for different types of paprika. Who even needs three kinds of paprika? Smoked, sweet, Hungarian... it's just red dust. \n\nBut anyway, she claims it helps her process her feelings, but she usually just ends up yelling at someone for putting the garlic powder next to the onion powder. Or not cope, since it doesn't work. \n\nIt reminds me of my roommate, honestly. His coping mechanism for literally anything is playing this one specific video game. It's this farming simulator thing, and whenever he bombs a test or gets in a fight with his girlfriend, he just sits there for hours, planting virtual turnips. He actually missed a midterm last month because his digital cow was giving birth or something. I tried to tell him that maybe he should focus on real life, but he got super defensive and told me I didn't understand the \"in-game economy.\" Which, fine, I don't, but I also don't think turnips are going to pass Chem 101 for him. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if I'm dealing with my emotions when I eat. I think I'm just trying to run away from them for a little bit, even if it ends up backfiring.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat as a way to intentionally distract yourself from feeling upset or stressed?\n\nPatient: Intentionally? It just sort of happens. \n\nIt is kind of like last semester when I had this massive history paper due on the Industrial Revolution. I knew I was super stressed about it, so I decided to take a break to clear my head. I thought I would just watch one episode of a documentary about deep sea squids. But then I went down this massive rabbit hole about giant squids and how they fight sperm whales, and suddenly it is four in the morning, and I am reading conspiracy theories about giant prehistoric sharks still living in the Mariana Trench. I ended up writing three pages of my history paper about maritime myths before I realized what I was doing. My professor actually left a note on my paper asking why I spent a whole paragraph talking about sea monsters in an essay about textile mills. \n\nSo yeah, I really do not know if I am eating on purpose to distract myself. I think my brain just kind of hijacks me when I am stressed and I end up doing things without really meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat as a way to avoid feeling your emotions?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'm trying to avoid them, exactly. It's more like... I just don't want to deal with them right then? Like, I'm not trying to run away forever, just hitting pause. \n\nKind of like how my grandpa used to \"fix\" the lawnmower. And sometimes it actually worked! But most of the time, it would just start smoking halfway through the yard, and he'd end up leaving it in the middle of the grass for a week. My grandma would get so mad because the neighbors would stare at this half-mowed lawn with a smoking machine just sitting there. I mean, I don't really know if it's coping. It's just... what happens. Like, I don't feel like I'm handling anything when I do it, you know? It's just a thing I end up doing. 

It kind of reminds me of my car. I have this old Honda Civic, and the check engine light has been on since I bought it from this guy on Craigslist. I took it to a mechanic once, and he told me it was probably just a loose gas cap. So I bought a new gas cap, but the light stayed on. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um, well, I came in because... lately, once I start eating, it feels like I just disappear. Like something else entirely takes over. It's kind of like autopilot, you know? Actually, it reminds me of this custom PC I was trying to build for my dorm—I'm taking a few college classes right now, well, mostly online because the parking on campus is a complete joke. They oversell the student permits by like fifty percent every semester, so you're just driving around in circles for an hour, and then you're late for your morning lecture anyway, so what's the point? \n\nBut anyway, yeah, with the food... it's like that. Even while I'm literally in my own head telling myself to stop, I just keep going and going until I can't stand it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have control over what or how much you're eating when this happens?\n\nPatient: Control? No. Zero. None at all. It's honestly like... you know when your controller disconnects right in the middle of a match? You're just mashing the buttons in your head and nothing is happening. \n\nWhich is the absolute worst, by the way. My roommate last semester, he'd always leave the PS5 controllers off the charging dock. Like, how hard is it to just click it into place when you're done? You literally just set it down. But no, he'd leave them on the couch, usually under a pillow or a hoodie or something, and then I'd log on to play with my friends from back home, and right as the game starts, boom, battery dead. We actually got knocked out of a tournament bracket once because of that guy. I was so mad... I think I actually didn't talk to him for a week. \n\nBut um, what were we talking about? Oh, right. The food. Yeah, I don't really have any say in what I'm eating or how much once that autopilot kicks in. I'm just kind of watching it happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um... I don't know if guilty is the right word. I mean, I guess I just don't really know how I feel after. I try not to think about it, honestly. It's like... you know when you watch a movie that's just completely terrible, and you get to the end, and you're just sitting there like, \"Well, that was a waste of two hours\"? \n\nSpeaking of terrible movies, have you seen that new sci-fi one on Netflix? The one with the aliens that look like giant squids? Man, the CGI in that was so bad. Like, early 2000s video game bad. I was watching it the other night because my internet was down and I couldn't get on Discord, which is super annoying by the way. My ISP is the worst. They always throttle my connection right around 8 PM, which is exactly when everyone gets online to play. I've called them like three times, and they always give me the same runaround about \"peak usage hours.\" It's a total scam. If I'm paying for gigabit speed, I should get gigabit speed, you know? \n\nBut yeah, I don't really know if I feel guilty. I just... I don't really know.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any issues with your sex life?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't think I have any issues with that. Honestly, I haven't even been trying to meet anyone lately anyway because my phone screen is completely shattered. I dropped it right outside the dining hall on Tuesday. Have you ever tried using a dating app when your screen looks like a literal spiderweb? It's impossible. You try to swipe on someone and end up clicking some weird pop-up ad for a mobile game. \n\nAnd getting it fixed is such a scam. I went to one of those little kiosks at the mall, and the guy wanted two hundred bucks just to replace the glass! Two hundred! I told him I could buy a decent used graphics card for that kind of money. Well, maybe not a top-tier one, the GPU market is still pretty wild right now. My buddy from my comp sci class tried to buy a used 3060 off eBay last month and the seller literally mailed him a brick in a cardboard box. Like, an actual red brick. eBay refunded him, obviously, but still, the hassle of it all is just ridiculous. \n\nBut um, yeah. No issues with my sex life or anything. Just... haven't really been focused on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty getting or maintaining an erection?\n\nPatient: Um, no? I mean, I don't think so. Like I said, I haven't really been doing anything where that would even come up. It's just not on my radar right now. \n\nHonestly, the only thing I'm trying to get up and running right now is my PC. I was trying to install this new mod for Skyrim the other day—have you ever played Skyrim? I know it's super old, but the modding community is still insane. Anyway, I downloaded this massive overhaul mod, like 40 gigabytes, and it completely broke my save file. Just corrupted the whole thing. I had like three hundred hours on that character. I was doing a pure mage build, which is actually really hard early game because your magicka regenerates so slowly and you basically have to run away from everything. I spent like two days trying to troubleshoot it, going through all the load orders and the text files, and nothing worked. I ended up having to wipe the whole drive and reinstall Windows just to be safe because my computer started making this weird clicking noise. \n\nWhich, by the way, I think might be the power supply failing. If I have to buy a new power supply on top of fixing my phone screen, I'm literally going to have to eat ramen for the rest of the semester. \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, no, no problems with that. Just... haven't been using it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in your interest in sex?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess? I mean, like I've been saying, I just haven't been interested in it at all lately. It's just not something I think about. \n\nHonestly, the only thing I've been interested in lately is trying to figure out how to fix my sleep schedule. It's completely messed up. I've been staying up until like 4 AM watching these YouTube video essays about speedrunning. Have you ever watched those? They're weirdly fascinating. There's this one guy who spent like three years trying to shave two seconds off a Super Mario 64 world record, and he literally mapped out the exact pixel coordinates of where Mario's foot needs to land on a specific frame. It's insane. It makes you realize how much free time some people have. \n\nWhich is ironic, because I feel like I have zero free time between classes and studying, even though I'm basically failing my chemistry lab. My lab partner is this guy who never shows up, and when he does, he just copies my data and leaves. I tried complaining to the TA, but she just told me that \"learning to work with difficult people is part of the college experience.\" Like, no, doing all the work for a group project while some guy named Chad gets an easy A is not the college experience I signed up for. \n\nBut yeah, sex just... isn't really on my mind. At all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits have anything to do with your lack of interest in sex?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know? I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess I just figure I'm too stressed out with everything else to care about dating or hooking up or whatever. \n\nLike, my eating habits... I don't know, it's just food, you know? It's not like food is what's keeping me from swiping on Tinder. It's more like... the whole dining hall situation is just so stressful anyway. They changed the meal plan system this semester, and now everything is on this weird point system instead of just swipes, and it makes zero sense. You get like, 500 points a week, but a single slice of pizza is somehow 12 points, and a salad is 18 points. Who prices a salad higher than pizza? It's like they want us to be unhealthy. And the app you have to use to check your balance crashes constantly. I tried to buy a coffee yesterday and stood at the register for five minutes while the little loading circle just spun around and around. The barista was looking at me like I was trying to steal a three-dollar iced latte. \n\nSo yeah, between the meal plan app being garbage and my phone screen being completely destroyed, I'm just... not really focused on anything else. I don't think food has anything to do with it, honestly. I'm just busy.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in sex might be related to how you've been feeling about your eating?\n\nPatient: I mean, I really don't think so. Like I said, it's just... I'm just not thinking about it. My eating is just my eating, you know? It's just something I have to deal with, like everything else. \n\nSpeaking of dealing with things, I actually have to figure out how I'm going to get my stuff out of my dorm at the end of the semester. Have you ever tried to pack up a whole dorm room into a two-door Honda Civic? Because that's what I'm looking at right now. My roommate, the one who leaves his wet towels on my desk chair—which is still happening, by the way, I literally sat on a damp towel yesterday while trying to write a paper on the French Revolution—anyway, he's getting his parents to bring an entire U-Haul just for his stuff. A U-Haul! For one half of a tiny room! He has this massive mini-fridge that he never even cleans out. I swear there's a yogurt in there from September that's probably achieved sentience by now. \n\nAnd my parents aren't coming to help me move out because my dad is weirdly obsessed with this new lawn care routine. He bought this aerator machine and he's been spending every weekend just punching holes in the grass. He sends me pictures of the lawn every Sunday morning. Like, \"Look at the soil drainage, son!\" I don't know what I'm supposed to say to that. \n\nSo yeah, I'm just going to have to cram everything into my car and hope I don't get pulled over because I won't be able to see out the back window. But no, I don't think my eating has anything to do with not wanting to hook up with anyone. I just have way too much other stuff going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat more when you're feeling stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess I do. Like, especially when I'm super stressed about classes. There have been nights after studying where I just... I don't know, I go from the dining hall back to my room and just eat. Like, burgers, bowls of cereal, peanut butter straight out of the jar, chips, literally whatever I can get my hands on. It's way more than a normal dinner, and I just keep going until the trash can is overflowing and I feel physically sick. \n\nIt's actually kind of a problem because my dorm trash can is one of those tiny plastic ones, you know? The ones that fit under a desk but can barely hold a single pizza box. I don't know why they give us those. And the main dumpster is all the way on the other side of the courtyard, which is just brilliant planning by the university. So when it rains, you have to trudge through this massive mud puddle just to throw away your garbage. \n\nLast week, I tried to take my trash out, and the bag ripped right at the bottom of the stairs. Just... garbage everywhere. And of course, it was right when the girl from my English seminar was walking by. The one who always wears those vintage band tees and has really strong opinions about Sylvia Plath. I had to awkwardly scoop up all these empty wrappers while she just stared at me. It was humiliating. \n\nBut yeah, I definitely eat a lot more when I'm stressed out. It just kind of happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating helps you feel better when you're stressed or upset?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't know if it actually helps. Maybe for like, a second? While I'm doing it, I'm not thinking about all the stuff I have to do, but then right after, I just feel gross and even more stressed out. \n\nIt's like when I tried to fix that stupid phone screen myself. I bought one of those DIY repair kits off Amazon because the guy at the mall kiosk wanted like, two hundred bucks, which is insane. So I get the kit, and it comes with these tiny little screwdrivers that look like they belong in a dollhouse. I spent three hours watching this YouTube tutorial by a guy who kept talking about his cryptocurrency investments while taking apart an iPhone. I finally got the screen off, but then I sneezed and lost one of the microscopic screws in the carpet. I spent another hour crawling around with a flashlight, and I never found it. So now my phone is held together with a piece of clear tape in the top corner, and the front camera is permanently blurry, which makes all my selfies look like they were taken in a steam room. \n\nSo yeah, I guess eating is kind of like that. It seems like a good idea to fix the stress in the moment, but then it just makes everything worse and messier.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat when you're stressed as a way to calm down or feel better?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? Like I said, it kind of distracts me for a minute, but I wouldn't say it actually calms me down. It's more just something I do. \n\nKind of like how my mom always starts aggressively organizing the pantry whenever she's mad at my dad. She doesn't even say anything, she just starts taking out all the canned goods and lining them up by expiration date. Last Thanksgiving, they got into an argument about whether to brine the turkey—my dad is anti-brine, which is a whole other thing—and next thing I know, my mom is in the kitchen with a label maker, reorganizing the spice rack. She made a separate section just for different types of paprika. Who even needs three kinds of paprika? Smoked, sweet, Hungarian... it's just red dust. \n\nBut anyway, she claims it helps her process her feelings, but she usually just ends up yelling at someone for putting the garlic powder next to the onion powder. So I guess people just have weird ways of dealing with stuff. But for me, eating just makes me feel worse afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat as a way to deal with your emotions?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, probably. Like, I don't really sit down and think, \"I am sad, so I will eat,\" but it's definitely tied to my emotions. It's just a way to cope, I guess? Or not cope, since it doesn't work. \n\nIt reminds me of my roommate, honestly. His coping mechanism for literally anything is playing this one specific video game. It's this farming simulator thing, and whenever he bombs a test or gets in a fight with his girlfriend, he just sits there for hours, planting virtual turnips. He actually missed a midterm last month because his digital cow was giving birth or something. I tried to tell him that maybe he should focus on real life, but he got super defensive and told me I didn't understand the \"in-game economy.\" Which, fine, I don't, but I also don't think turnips are going to pass Chem 101 for him. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if I'm dealing with my emotions when I eat. I think I'm just trying to run away from them for a little bit, even if it ends up backfiring.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat as a way to intentionally distract yourself from feeling upset or stressed?\n\nPatient: Intentionally? Uh, I am not really sure if it is a conscious choice, you know? Like I do not sit there and map out a plan to distract myself. It just sort of happens. \n\nIt is kind of like last semester when I had this massive history paper due on the Industrial Revolution. I knew I was super stressed about it, so I decided to take a break to clear my head. I thought I would just watch one episode of a documentary about deep sea squids. But then I went down this massive rabbit hole about giant squids and how they fight sperm whales, and suddenly it is four in the morning, and I am reading conspiracy theories about giant prehistoric sharks still living in the Mariana Trench. I ended up writing three pages of my history paper about maritime myths before I realized what I was doing. My professor actually left a note on my paper asking why I spent a whole paragraph talking about sea monsters in an essay about textile mills. \n\nSo yeah, I really do not know if I am eating on purpose to distract myself. I think my brain just kind of hijacks me when I am stressed and I end up doing things without really meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat as a way to avoid feeling your emotions?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'm trying to avoid them, exactly. It's more like... I just don't want to deal with them right then? Like, I'm not trying to run away forever, just hitting pause. \n\nKind of like how my grandpa used to \"fix\" the lawnmower. Every time it broke, he wouldn't actually take it apart to see what was wrong. He'd just hit it with a rubber mallet a few times, spray WD-40 on whatever part looked rusty, and hope for the best. And sometimes it actually worked! But most of the time, it would just start smoking halfway through the yard, and he'd end up leaving it in the middle of the grass for a week. My grandma would get so mad because the neighbors would stare at this half-mowed lawn with a smoking machine just sitting there. He eventually tried to paint it bright red to make it look newer, but he used indoor paint, so the first time it rained, it just peeled off in these weird, sticky strips. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if I'm avoiding my feelings. I think I just don't really know what to do with them, so I just... do something else instead.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating is a way for you to cope with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really know if it's coping. It's just... what happens. Like, I don't feel like I'm handling anything when I do it, you know? It's just a thing I end up doing. \n\nIt kind of reminds me of my car. I have this old Honda Civic, and the check engine light has been on since I bought it from this guy on Craigslist. I took it to a mechanic once, and he told me it was probably just a loose gas cap. So I bought a new gas cap, but the light stayed on. Then my buddy told me if I unhooked the battery for ten minutes, it would reset the computer and the light would go off. So I tried that, and it actually worked! For like, two days. Then it came back on, but this time the radio stopped working too. So now I just drive around in silence with this glowing orange engine symbol staring at me. Sometimes I put a piece of black electrical tape over it so I don't have to look at it, especially when I'm driving at night because it's super bright. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if eating is a coping mechanism. I think I just don't have a better way to deal with things right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. While I'm doing it, I'm not thinking about all the stuff I have to do, but then right after, I just feel gross and even more stressed out. \n\nIt's like when I tried to fix that stupid phone screen myself. I bought one of those DIY repair kits off Amazon because the guy at the mall kiosk wanted like, two hundred bucks, which is insane. So I get the kit, and it comes with these tiny little screwdrivers that look like they belong in a dollhouse. I spent three hours watching this YouTube tutorial by a guy who kept talking about his cryptocurrency investments while taking apart an iPhone. I finally got the screen off, but then I sneezed and lost one of the microscopic screws in the carpet. I spent another hour crawling around with a flashlight, and I never found it. So now my phone is held together with a piece of clear tape in the top corner, and the front camera is permanently blurry, which makes all my selfies look like they were taken in a steam room. \n\nSo yeah, I guess eating is kind of like that. It seems like a good idea to fix the stress in the moment, but then it just makes everything worse and messier.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat when you're stressed as a way to calm down or feel better?\n\nPatient: I don't know, maybe? Like I said, it kind of distracts me for a minute, but I wouldn't say it actually calms me down. It's more just something I do. \n\nKind of like how my mom always starts aggressively organizing the pantry whenever she's mad at my dad. She doesn't even say anything, she just starts taking out all the canned goods and lining them up by expiration date. Last Thanksgiving, they got into an argument about whether to brine the turkey—my dad is anti-brine, which is a whole other thing—and next thing I know, my mom is in the kitchen with a label maker, reorganizing the spice rack. She made a separate section just for different types of paprika. Who even needs three kinds of paprika? Smoked, sweet, Hungarian... it's just red dust. \n\nBut anyway, she claims it helps her process her feelings, but she usually just ends up yelling at someone for putting the garlic powder next to the onion powder. So I guess people just have weird ways of dealing with stuff. But for me, eating just makes me feel worse afterward.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat as a way to deal with your emotions?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, probably. Like, I don't really sit down and think, \"I am sad, so I will eat,\" but it's definitely tied to my emotions. It's just a way to cope, I guess? Or not cope, since it doesn't work. \n\nIt reminds me of my roommate, honestly. His coping mechanism for literally anything is playing this one specific video game. It's this farming simulator thing, and whenever he bombs a test or gets in a fight with his girlfriend, he just sits there for hours, planting virtual turnips. He actually missed a midterm last month because his digital cow was giving birth or something. I tried to tell him that maybe he should focus on real life, but he got super defensive and told me I didn't understand the \"in-game economy.\" Which, fine, I don't, but I also don't think turnips are going to pass Chem 101 for him. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if I'm dealing with my emotions when I eat. I think I'm just trying to run away from them for a little bit, even if it ends up backfiring.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat as a way to intentionally distract yourself from feeling upset or stressed?\n\nPatient: Intentionally? Uh, I am not really sure if it is a conscious choice, you know? Like I do not sit there and map out a plan to distract myself. It just sort of happens. \n\nIt is kind of like last semester when I had this massive history paper due on the Industrial Revolution. I knew I was super stressed about it, so I decided to take a break to clear my head. I thought I would just watch one episode of a documentary about deep sea squids. But then I went down this massive rabbit hole about giant squids and how they fight sperm whales, and suddenly it is four in the morning, and I am reading conspiracy theories about giant prehistoric sharks still living in the Mariana Trench. I ended up writing three pages of my history paper about maritime myths before I realized what I was doing. My professor actually left a note on my paper asking why I spent a whole paragraph talking about sea monsters in an essay about textile mills. \n\nSo yeah, I really do not know if I am eating on purpose to distract myself. I think my brain just kind of hijacks me when I am stressed and I end up doing things without really meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you eat as a way to avoid feeling your emotions?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'm trying to avoid them, exactly. It's more like... I just don't want to deal with them right then? Like, I'm not trying to run away forever, just hitting pause. \n\nKind of like how my grandpa used to \"fix\" the lawnmower. Every time it broke, he wouldn't actually take it apart to see what was wrong. He'd just hit it with a rubber mallet a few times, spray WD-40 on whatever part looked rusty, and hope for the best. And sometimes it actually worked! But most of the time, it would just start smoking halfway through the yard, and he'd end up leaving it in the middle of the grass for a week. My grandma would get so mad because the neighbors would stare at this half-mowed lawn with a smoking machine just sitting there. He eventually tried to paint it bright red to make it look newer, but he used indoor paint, so the first time it rained, it just peeled off in these weird, sticky strips. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if I'm avoiding my feelings. I think I just don't really know what to do with them, so I just... do something else instead.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating is a way for you to cope with difficult emotions?\n\nPatient: I mean, I don't really know if it's coping. So I bought a new gas cap, but the light stayed on. Then my buddy told me if I unhooked the battery for ten minutes, it would reset the computer and the light would go off. So I tried that, and it actually worked! For like, two days. Then it came back on, but this time the radio stopped working too. So now I just drive around in silence with this glowing orange engine symbol staring at me. Sometimes I put a piece of black electrical tape over it so I don't have to look at it, especially when I'm driving at night because it's super bright. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if eating is a coping mechanism. I don't think it's on purpose. Like, I'm not sitting there thinking, "Okay, I'm feeling bad, time to eat." It's more of a blur, honestly. I don't really have a plan or anything. 

It's sort of like when I tried to learn how to play the guitar last summer. I bought this cheap acoustic off a guy who lived in my dorm. He said it was a vintage Yamaha, but I'm pretty sure he just slapped a sticker on a beginner's guitar from Walmart. Anyway, I watched all these YouTube tutorials and tried to learn the chords for "Wonderwall" because, you know, everyone learns that one. But my fingers just wouldn't stretch the right way, and I ended up getting these terrible blisters. So I bought these little silicone finger protectors, which made it impossible to actually feel the strings. I eventually gave up and tried to turn the guitar into a planter for this fern my mom gave me, but the wood got all rotten and it smelled like a swamp in my room for a month. My roommate was so mad, he threatened to throw it out the window. 

So, I don't really know if I'm doing it on purpose. It's just something that happens before I even realize what's going on.
